> help
One action = one game-hour; queries take no time. Sleep keeps you in bed at least 4 hours (8 when you camp); wait to sleep on.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects use the previous hour's activity.
Out on open ground with bedding, sleep makes camp where you stand; wait follows your lodging order, returning to a nearby paid or own bed when available.
look | signs | investigate <sign id> | actions [page] | go <place name/id> | talk <person> | <interaction> <person>
buy <item> [qty] | sell <item> [qty] | eat <item> [servings] [method] | cook <item> [servings] [method]
drink | meal | sleep | rest | work | wait <Nh|Nd> | wait until <HH:00|dawn|morning|noon|dusk|night|tomorrow>
prices | places [page] | standing <sleep|eat|drink|warmth|lodging> <on|off> | agency <commanded|psyche>
inspect <person/item/place> | pack <item> into <container> (moves one) | unpack <item>
aid <person> | retire | depart | rations <10..150> | plan | journal (lists what you owe) | explain <event id>
Enter a menu number or stable action id. help

  Amos Whitcomb: “Queer how?”

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 04:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 04:00 A report from Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door.
1855-04-01 07:00 Sunday. Joel Pike insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I kept out of it, and am not certain I am proud of that.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.

  Ellis Wayfarer: “Ground's drying out at last.”
  Hester Fenn: “Time to be getting seed in, then, and no more excuses.”
  Jory Trevenna: “I don't think I know your face. Jory Trevenna.”
  Abigail Hatch: “Abigail Hatch. Nor I yours, but that's mended now.”
  Jory Trevenna: “Will you pray with me, Mrs. Hatch?”
  Abigail Hatch: “Gladly. Our Father, which art in heaven...”
  Hester Fenn: “So there I was, alone on the bluff, with the wolves all round...”
  Amos Whitcomb: “You told it different last week. There were fewer wolves.”

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 04:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 04:00 A report from Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door.
1855-04-01 07:00 Sunday. Joel Pike insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I kept out of it, and am not certain I am proud of that.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 15:00 Johann Brenner made sport of Hester Fenn at Dunleavy's Tavern, and I was by to see it. Everyone pretended to be looking at something else.
Since last look: no time passed.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 04:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 04:00 A report from Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door.
1855-04-01 07:00 Sunday. Joel Pike insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I kept out of it, and am not certain I am proud of that.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 15:00 Johann Brenner made sport of Hester Fenn at Dunleavy's Tavern, and I was by to see it. Everyone pretended to be looking at something else.
1855-04-01 16:00 Mr. J. Brenner insulted Mrs. N. Dunleavy to the face at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon. The company took sides, and the story was round the village by supper.
1855-04-01 16:00 Johann Brenner insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon. I was by to see it.
1855-04-01 16:00 Johann Brenner drank a toast with Ragnhild Haugen at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon.
1855-04-01 21:00 Michael Dunleavy lent money to Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss.
1855-04-02 07:00 Nora Dunleavy settled with Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the forenoon. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-02 10:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the forenoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 11:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the forenoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 12:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 12:00 Amos Whitcomb told a tale to Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday at noon.
1855-04-02 12:00 Loveday Trevenna helped Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 13:00 Captain Trevenna was in close talk with Loveday Trevenna at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 Joel Pike helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-03 12:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 12:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 18:00 Amos Whitcomb helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-03 19:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous. Snow in the morning.
1855-04-04 05:00 Wednesday. Michael Dunleavy slighted Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern. I kept out of it, and am not certain I am proud of that.
1855-04-04 21:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-06 00:00 Friday, the 6th of April, 1855; the waning gibbous.
Since last look: no time passed.

[14597] Lydia Pratt: Comfort ▸ Gussie Pratt (it went well)

  Gussie Pratt: “Much obliged. I needed to hear it.”

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 04:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 04:00 A report from Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door.
1855-04-01 07:00 Sunday. Joel Pike insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I kept out of it, and am not certain I am proud of that.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 15:00 Johann Brenner made sport of Hester Fenn at Dunleavy's Tavern, and I was by to see it. Everyone pretended to be looking at something else.
1855-04-01 16:00 Mr. J. Brenner insulted Mrs. N. Dunleavy to the face at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon. The company took sides, and the story was round the village by supper.
1855-04-01 16:00 Johann Brenner insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon. I was by to see it.
1855-04-01 16:00 Johann Brenner drank a toast with Ragnhild Haugen at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon.
1855-04-01 21:00 Michael Dunleavy lent money to Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss.
1855-04-02 07:00 Nora Dunleavy settled with Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the forenoon. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-02 10:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the forenoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 11:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the forenoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 12:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 12:00 Amos Whitcomb told a tale to Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday at noon.
1855-04-02 12:00 Loveday Trevenna helped Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 13:00 Captain Trevenna was in close talk with Loveday Trevenna at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 Joel Pike helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-03 12:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 12:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 18:00 Amos Whitcomb helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-03 19:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous. Snow in the morning.
1855-04-04 05:00 Wednesday. Michael Dunleavy slighted Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern. I kept out of it, and am not certain I am proud of that.
1855-04-04 21:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-06 00:00 Friday, the 6th of April, 1855; the waning gibbous.
1855-04-06 08:00 Lydia Pratt comforted Gussie Pratt at Pratt & Daughter, Outfitters. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-06 10:00 Gussie Pratt helped Lydia Pratt at Pratt & Daughter, Outfitters. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-07 00:00 Saturday, the 7th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous. Rain in the night.
1855-04-07 00:00 I am down with want of food. Too long on salt meat and meal; the body keeps its own books.
1855-04-07 01:00 Joel Pike comforted me at Dunleavy's Tavern on Saturday in the small hours, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-07 12:00 Michael Dunleavy borrowed of Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-07 20:00 Harriet Glover helped Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-07 22:00 Michael Dunleavy sat with me when I was low, and would not be thanked for it. I shall not forget it.
1855-04-07 00:00 Week 1. The house took 1795 calories a head by the day, near enough, on hardtack (ship's biscuit), dried apples and pemmican. Set down also: nothing worth the naming gone.
1855-04-08 00:00 Sunday, the 8th of April, 1855. The Sabbath, and the last quarter. Rain in the morning.
1855-04-08 05:00 Michael Dunleavy talked long and low with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday at first light.
1855-04-08 06:00 Michael Dunleavy comforted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-08 07:00 Michael Dunleavy talked with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the forenoon.
Since last look: no time passed.
